========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… I mostly tell myself I’m just wondering, because I know there isn’t clear proof. I don’t want to say I’m certain and sound unreasonable.

But honestly, even on my better days, I can’t always shake it. When it happens at church or with the walking club, it feels like people are watching me, or like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to talk myself out of it, but part of me still believes it might really be directed at me.

I usually keep it to myself… mostly. I don’t want people thinking I’m accusing them or making something out of nothing.

I know that may sound like I’m overthinking it, and maybe I am, but in the moment I felt pretty sure it was meant for me.\n\nDoctor: When you have these feelings that messages are directed at you, do you find that you believe them strongly even when there’s no clear proof, or do you mostly just wonder if it might be true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mostly tell myself I’m just wondering, because I know there isn’t clear proof. I don’t want to say I’m certain and sound unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, even on my better days, I can’t always shake it. When it happens at church or with the walking club, it feels like people are watching me, or like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to talk myself out of it, but part of me still believes it might really be directed at me.\n\nDoctor: When you think these messages are directed at you, do you ever talk to someone else about it to see if they notice the same thing, or do you usually keep it to yourself because you feel others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: I usually keep it to myself… mostly. I don’t want people thinking I’m accusing them or making something out of nothing.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, I guess. Even on days when I’m doing alright, I still can’t always shake this feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I know that sounds… maybe a little odd. I just haven’t felt comfortable asking other people if they see it too, because I’m afraid they wouldn’t understand.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about messages being directed at you, do you ever find yourself completely sure it’s true, even if there’s no real evidence, or do you mostly just have doubts and wonder if it could be true?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely sure… I mean, I know there isn’t real evidence, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it’s more than just a passing doubt, I guess. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading into it, but part of me still feels like it could really be about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual thoughts or beliefs that most people might not understand, such as thinking that your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of you, or that you have special abilities or a special mission?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like… special powers or a mission or anything like that. Even when I’m having a pretty decent day, I still get this feeling I can’t quite shake that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that may sound strange, and I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but that’s the main unusual thing.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about people sending messages about you, do you ever feel that they are part of a larger pattern or plan that others don’t see, like a hidden conspiracy or something more organized?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a conspiracy, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings mostly happened when you’re feeling down, stressed, or in a certain mood, or do they seem to come up regardless of how you’re feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say it’s only when I’m down or stressed, because that would make more sense, I guess… but it hasn’t been only then.\n\nThere was a stretch for several weeks where I was doing a bit better — getting dressed, eating a little better, not feeling so weighed down — and even then I still heard my name in the house sometimes, and I still had that feeling that people at church were hinting about me. But when I’m at church or with the walking club, I get this feeling that certain comments, or little things in the bulletin, are about me somehow… like they’re trying to say something without saying it directly. I know other people might not see it that way, but I have a hard time shaking it.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are sending messages about you, do you ever feel like these messages are specifically directed at you, even when others don’t seem to notice them or agree that they’re there? Like they might be trying to send you a secret signal or talking about you in code?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not all the time, and I don’t want to sound like I’m making a big thing out of it. But sometimes it really does feel like certain little comments, or even something in the church bulletin, are meant for me somehow. Like they’re not saying my name, but it feels pointed at me.\n\nAnd with the walking club too, if people say something in a certain way, I can get this feeling that they’re watching me or talking around me. I know other people might not notice anything, so maybe I’m just being too sensitive… but it’s hard for me to shake when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific time when you felt a message or comment was directed at you? What happened, and how did you know it was about you?\n\nPatient: Well… one example is at church. I remember looking at the bulletin and there was a little note in there, and I can’t even say it named me or anything like that, but it felt like it was about me. Like the wording was just… pointed somehow.\n\nAnd then afterward, when a couple people made comments in passing, I had this feeling they were watching how I reacted, or saying things around me instead of directly to me. I know that may sound like I’m overthinking it, and maybe I am, but in the moment I felt pretty sure it was meant for me.\n\nDoctor: When you have these feelings that messages are directed at you, do you find that you believe them strongly even when there’s no clear proof, or do you mostly just wonder if it might be true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mostly tell myself I’m just wondering, because I know there isn’t clear proof. I don’t want to say I’m certain and sound unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, even on my better days, I can’t always shake it. When it happens at church or with the walking club, it feels like people are watching me, or like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to talk myself out of it, but part of me still believes it might really be directed at me.\n\nDoctor: When you think these messages are directed at you, do you ever talk to someone else about it to see if they notice the same thing, or do you usually keep it to yourself because you feel others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: I usually keep it to myself… mostly. I don’t want people thinking I’m accusing them or making something out of nothing.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, I guess. Even on days when I’m doing alright, I still can’t always shake this feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I know that sounds… maybe a little odd. I just haven’t felt comfortable asking other people if they see it too, because I’m afraid they wouldn’t understand.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about messages being directed at you, do you ever find yourself completely sure it’s true, even if there’s no real evidence, or do you mostly just have doubts and wonder if it could be true?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely sure… I mean, I know there isn’t real evidence, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it’s more than just a passing doubt, I guess. I came in because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading into it, but part of me still feels like it could really be about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual thoughts or beliefs that most people might not understand, such as thinking that your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of you, or that you have special abilities or a special mission?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like… special powers or a mission or anything like that. And I don’t feel like something is controlling my thoughts.\n\nIt’s more the thing I mentioned, I guess. Even when I’m having a pretty decent day, I still get this feeling I can’t quite shake that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that may sound strange, and I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but that’s the main unusual thing.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about people sending messages about you, do you ever feel that they are part of a larger pattern or plan that others don’t see, like a hidden conspiracy or something more organized?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a conspiracy, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. I’m not trying to say there’s some organized plan.\n\nIt’s more like… even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things people say. I know that may not make sense, and maybe I’m reading into it, but it can feel connected somehow. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings mostly happened when you’re feeling down, stressed, or in a certain mood, or do they seem to come up regardless of how you’re feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say it’s only when I’m down or stressed, because that would make more sense, I guess… but it hasn’t been only then.\n\nThere was a stretch for several weeks where I was doing a bit better — getting dressed, eating a little better, not feeling so weighed down — and even then I still heard my name in the house sometimes, and I still had that feeling that people at church were hinting about me. Not all the time, and I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it was still there. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard sounds or voices that others around you don’t seem to hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… not all the time. It’s usually when the house is quiet. I’ll hear my name, or just a few words, and it sounds like my friend’s voice, maybe from the kitchen or the porch.\n\nIt can be clear enough that I answer, and then I remember nobody’s there. I know that probably sounds odd. I don’t want to make too much of it, but yes, that has happened.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so, at least a little. I don’t want to make it sound stranger than it is, but sometimes ordinary things feel like they’re somehow about me.

Like at church, I’ll see something in the bulletin and it feels like there’s a little message in there meant for me, or someone in the walking club will say something normal and I’ll wonder if they’re hinting about me. And I get this feeling that people are watching me.

But when I’m at church or with the walking club, I get this feeling that certain comments, or little things in the bulletin, are about me somehow… like they’re trying to say something without saying it directly. Like they might be trying to send you a secret signal or talking about you in code?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, not all the time, and I don’t want to sound like I’m making a big thing out of it. But sometimes it really does feel like certain little comments, or even something in the church bulletin, are meant for me somehow. Like they’re not saying my name, but it feels pointed at me.\n\nAnd with the walking club too, if people say something in a certain way, I can get this feeling that they’re watching me or talking around me. I know other people might not notice anything, so maybe I’m just being too sensitive… but it’s hard for me to shake when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific time when you felt a message or comment was directed at you? What happened, and how did you know it was about you?\n\nPatient: Well… one example is at church. I remember looking at the bulletin and there was a little note in there, and I can’t even say it named me or anything like that, but it felt like it was about me. Like the wording was just… pointed somehow.\n\nAnd then afterward, when a couple people made comments in passing, I had this feeling they were watching how I reacted, or saying things around me instead of directly to me. I know that may sound like I’m overthinking it, and maybe I am, but in the moment I felt pretty sure it was meant for me.\n\nDoctor: When you have these feelings that messages are directed at you, do you find that you believe them strongly even when there’s no clear proof, or do you mostly just wonder if it might be true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mostly tell myself I’m just wondering, because I know there isn’t clear proof. I don’t want to say I’m certain and sound unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, even on my better days, I can’t always shake it. When it happens at church or with the walking club, it feels like people are watching me, or like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to talk myself out of it, but part of me still believes it might really be directed at me.\n\nDoctor: When you think these messages are directed at you, do you ever talk to someone else about it to see if they notice the same thing, or do you usually keep it to yourself because you feel others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: I usually keep it to myself… mostly. I don’t want people thinking I’m accusing them or making something out of nothing.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, I guess. Even on days when I’m doing alright, I still can’t always shake this feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I know that sounds… maybe a little odd. I just haven’t felt comfortable asking other people if they see it too, because I’m afraid they wouldn’t understand.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about messages being directed at you, do you ever find yourself completely sure it’s true, even if there’s no real evidence, or do you mostly just have doubts and wonder if it could be true?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely sure… I mean, I know there isn’t real evidence, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it’s more than just a passing doubt, I guess. I came in because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading into it, but part of me still feels like it could really be about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual thoughts or beliefs that most people might not understand, such as thinking that your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of you, or that you have special abilities or a special mission?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like… special powers or a mission or anything like that. I know that may sound strange, and I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but that’s the main unusual thing.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about people sending messages about you, do you ever feel that they are part of a larger pattern or plan that others don’t see, like a hidden conspiracy or something more organized?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a conspiracy, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to love my garden, quilting, and reading before bed, but now I mostly leave the quilt basket ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I’ve done anything dramatic, really… I mean, I still live at home and I manage. But I guess I have changed some things.

I’ve stopped going to the quilting gatherings like I used to, and I don’t keep up with the walking club much anymore. I used to garden more too, and now I let a lot of it go. Part of it is just… it feels easier to stay home than wonder what people are thinking or if something is aimed at me.

My daughter has started checking in more, too—making sure I’ve eaten and that I remember appointments. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t take care of myself, because I try to, but she helps more than she used to. Is that what you mean by acting on it?

I know that may sound like I’m overthinking it, and maybe I am, but in the moment I felt pretty sure it was meant for me.\n\nDoctor: When you have these feelings that messages are directed at you, do you find that you believe them strongly even when there’s no clear proof, or do you mostly just wonder if it might be true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mostly tell myself I’m just wondering, because I know there isn’t clear proof. I don’t want to say I’m certain and sound unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, even on my better days, I can’t always shake it. I don’t want people thinking I’m accusing them or making something out of nothing.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, I guess. I know that sounds… maybe a little odd. I know that may sound strange, and I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but that’s the main unusual thing.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about people sending messages about you, do you ever feel that they are part of a larger pattern or plan that others don’t see, like a hidden conspiracy or something more organized?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a conspiracy, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings mostly happened when you’re feeling down, stressed, or in a certain mood, or do they seem to come up regardless of how you’re feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say it’s only when I’m down or stressed, because that would make more sense, I guess… but it hasn’t been only then.\n\nThere was a stretch for several weeks where I was doing a bit better — getting dressed, eating a little better, not feeling so weighed down — and even then I still heard my name in the house sometimes, and I still had that feeling that people at church were hinting about me. I just haven’t felt comfortable asking other people if they see it too, because I’m afraid they wouldn’t understand.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about messages being directed at you, do you ever find yourself completely sure it’s true, even if there’s no real evidence, or do you mostly just have doubts and wonder if it could be true?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely sure… I mean, I know there isn’t real evidence, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it’s more than just a passing doubt, I guess. I came in because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading into it, but part of me still feels like it could really be about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual thoughts or beliefs that most people might not understand, such as thinking that your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of you, or that you have special abilities or a special mission?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like… special powers or a mission or anything like that. And I don’t feel like something is controlling my thoughts.\n\nIt’s more the thing I mentioned, I guess. Even when I’m having a pretty decent day, I still get this feeling I can’t quite shake that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that may sound strange, and I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but that’s the main unusual thing.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about people sending messages about you, do you ever feel that they are part of a larger pattern or plan that others don’t see, like a hidden conspiracy or something more organized?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a conspiracy, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. I’m not trying to say there’s some organized plan.\n\nIt’s more like… even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things people say. I know that may not make sense, and maybe I’m reading into it, but it can feel connected somehow. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings mostly happened when you’re feeling down, stressed, or in a certain mood, or do they seem to come up regardless of how you’re feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say it’s only when I’m down or stressed, because that would make more sense, I guess… but it hasn’t been only then.\n\nThere was a stretch for several weeks where I was doing a bit better — getting dressed, eating a little better, not feeling so weighed down — and even then I still heard my name in the house sometimes, and I still had that feeling that people at church were hinting about me. Not all the time, and I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it was still there. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard sounds or voices that others around you don’t seem to hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… not all the time. It’s usually when the house is quiet. I’ll hear my name, or just a few words, and it sounds like my friend’s voice, maybe from the kitchen or the porch.\n\nIt can be clear enough that I answer, and then I remember nobody’s there. I know that probably sounds odd. I don’t want to make too much of it, but yes, that has happened.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like people are sending messages about you, do you ever feel like even ordinary things in the environment—like objects, signs, or events—are somehow specifically related to you in a way that others wouldn't understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, at least a little. I don’t want to make it sound stranger than it is, but sometimes ordinary things feel like they’re somehow about me.\n\nLike at church, I’ll see something in the bulletin and it feels like there’s a little message in there meant for me, or someone in the walking club will say something normal and I’ll wonder if they’re hinting about me. And I get this feeling that people are watching me.\n\nI know other people might not see it that way, and maybe I’m reading too much into it, but it can be hard to shake. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you had these experiences—like hearing your name or feeling like messages were directed at you—when you were feeling emotionally neutral or even happy, not just when you were stressed or down?\n\nPatient: Yes… I can think of a time like that. There was a spell for several weeks where I actually was doing a little better — I was getting dressed more, eating a bit better, and I didn’t feel quite so weighed down.\n\nBut even then, I still heard my name in the house sometimes. And at church, I still had that feeling like people were hinting about me, or that something being said was sort of meant for me.\n\nIt wasn’t constant, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was… but yes, it did happen when I wasn’t especially upset or down. Is that the kind of example you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever acted on any of these feelings — like changing your behavior, avoiding certain people, or doing something because you believed a message was meant for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve done anything dramatic, really… I mean, I still live at home and I manage. But I guess I have changed some things.\n\nI’ve stopped going to the quilting gatherings like I used to, and I don’t keep up with the walking club much anymore. I used to garden more too, and now I let a lot of it go. Part of it is just… it feels easier to stay home than wonder what people are thinking or if something is aimed at me.\n\nMy daughter has started checking in more, too—making sure I’ve eaten and that I remember appointments. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t take care of myself, because I try to, but she helps more than she used to. Is that what you mean by acting on it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What happened, and how did you know it was about you?\n\nPatient: Well… one example is at church. I remember looking at the bulletin and there was a little note in there, and I can’t even say it named me or anything like that, but it felt like it was about me. Like the wording was just… pointed somehow.\n\nAnd then afterward, when a couple people made comments in passing, I had this feeling they were watching how I reacted, or saying things around me instead of directly to me. I know that may sound like I’m overthinking it, and maybe I am, but in the moment I felt pretty sure it was meant for me.\n\nDoctor: When you have these feelings that messages are directed at you, do you find that you believe them strongly even when there’s no clear proof, or do you mostly just wonder if it might be true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mostly tell myself I’m just wondering, because I know there isn’t clear proof. I don’t want to say I’m certain and sound unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, even on my better days, I can’t always shake it. When it happens at church or with the walking club, it feels like people are watching me, or like there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to talk myself out of it, but part of me still believes it might really be directed at me.\n\nDoctor: When you think these messages are directed at you, do you ever talk to someone else about it to see if they notice the same thing, or do you usually keep it to yourself because you feel others wouldn’t understand?\n\nPatient: I usually keep it to myself… mostly. I don’t want people thinking I’m accusing them or making something out of nothing.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, I guess. Even on days when I’m doing alright, I still can’t always shake this feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I know that sounds… maybe a little odd. I just haven’t felt comfortable asking other people if they see it too, because I’m afraid they wouldn’t understand.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about messages being directed at you, do you ever find yourself completely sure it’s true, even if there’s no real evidence, or do you mostly just have doubts and wonder if it could be true?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m completely sure… I mean, I know there isn’t real evidence, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it’s more than just a passing doubt, I guess. I came in because even when I’m having a better day, I still can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. I try to tell myself maybe I’m reading into it, but part of me still feels like it could really be about me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual thoughts or beliefs that most people might not understand, such as thinking that your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of you, or that you have special abilities or a special mission?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like… special powers or a mission or anything like that. And I don’t feel like something is controlling my thoughts.\n\nIt’s more the thing I mentioned, I guess. Even when I’m having a pretty decent day, I still get this feeling I can’t quite shake that people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in what someone says. I know that may sound strange, and I’m not trying to make it into something bigger than it is, but that’s the main unusual thing.\n\nDoctor: When you have these thoughts about people sending messages about you, do you ever feel that they are part of a larger pattern or plan that others don’t see, like a hidden conspiracy or something more organized?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a conspiracy, exactly… that sounds bigger than what I mean. I’m not trying to say there’s some organized plan.\n\nIt’s more like… even on my better days, I can’t quite shake the feeling that some people at church or in the walking club are watching me, or that there are little messages about me in the bulletin or in things people say. I know that may not make sense, and maybe I’m reading into it, but it can feel connected somehow.
